# Of Bodies in General.
<meta>catchword: As</meta>

Such a union of all the bodies of the elect <note>original German: *Ausserwehlten* — those chosen by God for salvation.</note> into a single body has now been quite artfully and masterfully depicted by the Holy Scriptures of the Old and New Testaments—according to the mystical understanding thereof—through the **crown** which shall be placed upon the chosen. This same crown has been given various glorious names and titles according to the many-faceted majesty of the transfigured body. The wise King **Solomon** calls it <margin>4 Esdras 2:42 & following.</margin> <margin>Proverbs 4:9.</margin> an **illustrious crown** <note>original Latin: *Coronam inclytam*</note>, an excellent crown. The prophet **Ezekiel** calls it <margin>Ezekiel 16:12.</margin> a **crown of beauty** <note>original Latin: *Coronam decoris*</note>, a very decorative crown. The wise <margin>Wisdom of Solomon 5:17.</margin> man calls it a **diadem of splendor** <note>original Latin: *diadema speciei*</note>, a most glorious crown. The prophet **Isaiah** calls it a <margin>Isaiah 28:5.</margin> **lovely crown**, and speaks of it thus: *At that time the LORD of Hosts shall be a lovely crown and a glorious wreath for the remnant of His people.* And the Apostle **Peter** calls it the **crown of glory** <note>original Latin: *Coronam gloriæ*</note>. And he writes of it with these words: <margin>1 Peter 5:4.</margin> *When the Chief Shepherd shall appear, then you shall receive the unfading crown of honor.* Just as the Apostle **Paul** likewise calls it an **incorruptible crown** <note>original Latin: *Coronam incorruptibilem*</note>, a non-destructible or imperishable <margin>1 Corinthians 9:25</margin> crown, and (elsewhere) a **crown of righteousness** <note>original Latin: *Coronam justitiæ*</note>, <margin>2 Timothy 4:8.</margin> which the righteous Judge shall give to all those who love His appearing. In the **Revelation of John**, it is called the <margin>Revelation 2:10</margin> **crown of life** <note>original Latin: *Corona vitæ*</note>. As the Apostle **James** also <margin>James 1:12.</margin> names it, saying: *God has promised the crown of life to those who love Him.*

<summary>This page explores the biblical metaphor of the "Crown" as a symbol for the unified and glorified state of believers in the afterlife. It compiles numerous scriptural titles for this crown—such as the crown of glory, righteousness, and life—to illustrate the honor and permanence of the transfigured body.</summary>
<keywords>crown of life, crown of glory, the elect, transfigured body, Solomon, Ezekiel, Isaiah, Peter, Paul, James, mystical union, afterlife</keywords>

# Of Bodies in General.

Just as it was mentioned above in the 47th Chapter that the new bodies of the elect <note>chosen by God for salvation</note> will be very bright and shining: so too will the entire <term>harmonious body</term> <note>original Latin: *Harmonicum corpus* — a term used here to describe the Church or the collective assembly of believers as a single, perfectly tuned and unified body</note> of the community of God be entirely bright and shining in the future life. This was indicated in the Old Testament by the wise King Solomon in his Song of Songs with these words: 
<margin>Song of Solomon 6:9.</margin>
Who is she that breaks forth like the morning dawn, beautiful as the moon, choice as the sun, terrible as the points of an army? And the prophet Isaiah (who understands the chosen community of God by the name Jerusalem) says that her 
<margin>Isaiah 62:1.</margin>
righteousness shall go forth as a radiance, and her salvation shall burn like a torch. In the New Testament (namely, in the Revelation of John), this is 
<margin>Revelation 15:2.</margin>
quite elegantly indicated by the sea of glass, mingled with fire, by which the elect of God stood.

### That the Elect in that life shall see GOD face to face, behold Him, and recognize Him right perfectly.

That the first man, **Adam**, before the Fall and while he was still in his state of perfection, was able to see and behold **GOD** the Lord—of this there is no doubt, especially since Holy Scripture also indicates as much.

## Chapter 53.
# Summary Conclusion,

->In which the most prominent aspects of everything<-
->that has been cited in this entire treatise<-
->are briefly repeated, and proven word for word<-
->from the Holy Scripture.<-

---

<margin>
a) John 1:18 & 1 John 4:12; 1 Tim. 6:16.
b) Exod. 33:20; Sirach 43:35 <note>The German citation "Syr." refers to the Wisdom of Sirach, also known as Ecclesiasticus.</note>.
c) 2 Cor. 2:14.
d) John 4:24; 2 Cor. 3:17.
e) Exod. 3:14 & Isa. 43:10-11; Jer. 7:11.
f) Rom. 11:36.
g) 1 Cor. 15:28; 12:6.
</margin>
It is true that no one has ever seen **GOD**, a) just as it is also not possible for a human to see **GOD** in this life; b) we also may have only a *fragrance of the knowledge of God* c) <note>original: *odorem notitiæ Dei*</note> here. Therefore, in this place, we wish to report briefly from the Divine Scripture alone (insofar as **GOD** has revealed Himself and His essence therein). The Holy Scripture testifies of **GOD**, however, that He is a Spirit, d) who is substantial and exists of Himself, or has His being, e) and from Him, and through Him, and in Him all things are or exist. f) Yes, He is all in all, g) and with Him is the fountain of life; h) He is a **LORD** over life and spirit: i) and a **GOD** of the spirits of all flesh, k) from whom everything else (that lives) receives its life and breath, l) just as we humans live, move, and have our being in Him. m) <note>The German "leben, weben und schweben" is a poetic rendering of Acts 17:28; while "weben" and "schweben" literally mean "weave" and "soar/hover," in this context they signify the totality of human existence and movement within the Divine.</note>

---

<margin>
h) Ps. 36:10; 42:3.
i) 2 Macc. 14:46.
k) Num. 16:22; Job 12:10.
l) 1 Tim. 6:13; John 6:33; 1 Cor. 8:6; Eph. 4:6.
m) Acts 17:28; 1 Tim. 6:13.
</margin>

And if then we (the chosen) shall all d) be united in one body (of which body e) Christ shall be the head); then through him (Christ) as the mediator between God and men, f) we shall also be united g) with God himself and made partakers of the <term>divine nature</term> <note>The concept that believers share in the life and character of God.</note>; h) thus our fellowship shall be with the Father and his Son Jesus Christ; i) that same thing is the glory <note>The original text uses "Herzlichkeit," which literally means "heartfeltness," but in this 17th-century context, it is often a synonymous variant for "Herrlichkeit" (glory), matching the cited scripture in John 17.</note> which the Lord Christ will give us: k) namely, that we shall be one, just as the Father and Son are one; Christ in us,

<summary>This page describes the ultimate union of the elect into a single spiritual body headed by Christ, fulfilling biblical promises of restoration and divine fellowship. It emphasizes that this unity is facilitated through the sacraments and Christ’s role as the mediator between humanity and God.</summary>

<keywords>mystical body of Christ, the elect, vine and branches, sacraments, divine nature, mediator, Isaiah 58, spiritual union</keywords>

# Summary Conclusion.

To which eternal King / a) the immortal and invisible / and only wise / be honor and praise into eternity / A M E N.

### Isaiah 34:16.

Now seek diligently in the book of the Lord / and read: Not one of these shall be missing. Neither shall one miss this or that: For it is He / who commands through my mouth / and it is His Spirit / that brings it together. <note>The author uses this verse to validate that the spiritual truths described in this book are not his own inventions, but are gathered by the Holy Spirit from the "Book of the Lord."</note>

---

### Acts 17:11 & 12.

The most noble of those at Thessalonica <note>The text refers to the residents of Beroea who were "more noble" than those in Thessalonica, a common biblical example of diligent study.</note> received the word (from Paul and Silas) quite willingly; and searched daily in the Scripture / whether it were so. Thus many of them believed: Also of the honorable Greek women and men not a few.

---

### Psalm 95:7 & 8; Hebrews 3:7 & 8.

The Holy Spirit speaks: Today / if you will hear His voice / then do not harden your hearts. As happened in the provocation <note>original: "Verbitterung," meaning bitterness or rebellion.</note> on the day of testing in the wilderness.

<meta>This page continues the series of scriptural and authoritative proofs that conclude the main body of the work. Following the biblical citations on the previous page, the author now quotes the Old Testament prophet Hosea and two highly influential Early Church Fathers, Lactantius and Augustine, to reinforce the necessity of spiritual discernment over worldly tradition.</meta>

<page-num>230</page-num>

->Hosea 14:10.<-

Who is wise / let him understand this / and prudent / let him notice this: For the ways of the LORD are right / and the righteous walk in them; but the transgressors fall in them.

->*Lactantius, Divine Institutes, book 3, chapter 9.*<-

He who wishes to be wise and blessed / should hear the voice of God: Let him learn Righteousness: Let him know the mystery <note>original: "Sacramentum"</note> of his own Birth <note>In this context, this refers to the spiritual "new birth" or the origin of the soul.</note>: Let him despise human things: Let him embrace divine things / so that he might attain that Supreme Good <term>Summum Bonum: a philosophical and theological term referring to the highest, most ultimate good that a human being can achieve.</term> / (for which he was born): after all [false] religions are dissolved and everything (whatever used to be or could be said in their defense) is refuted.

->*Augustine, from his sermon on the Nativity of the Lord.*<-

Let no one discuss the heavenly mystery with earthly tradition: Let no one treat the Secret <term>Arcanum: a profound mystery or hidden knowledge accessible only to the initiated.</term> of the Birth <note>original: "Nativitatis," referring here to the Incarnation of Christ.</note> out of common habit: Let no one look for examples in that which is unique: Let no one devise a false accusation against Piety.

->*The same author, on the Agreement of the Four Evangelists.*<-

Not always, to all, nor in equal—

<meta>catchword: -ly,</meta>

<summary>This page presents authoritative quotes from the Prophet Hosea and Church Fathers Lactantius and Augustine, emphasizing that divine wisdom and the "Supreme Good" can only be understood through God's voice rather than human tradition.</summary>

<page-num>231</page-num>

—ly, is it persuaded <note>original: "persuadetur," referring to the Mystery of God mentioned in the parenthetical note</note> (namely, the Mystery of God) except to those alone, to whom God speaks inwardly.

### *Also on the Trinity.*

That Supreme Good <term>Summum Bonum: the ultimate objective of human life; in Christian theology, this is identified with God.</term> (which is perceived by the most purified minds) cannot be seen or understood by some: because the weak vision of the human mind is not fixed upon such excellent Light: unless it is first nourished and quickened through the Righteousness of faith.

### *The same author <note>Referring again to Augustine.</note> on Psalm 117.*

It is a great judgment of Piety and Holiness to believe, before you even know what has been said, that it ought to have been said exactly as it was. For this Piety makes you capable of seeking what was said: so that when you have sought it, you may find it; and when you have found it, you may rejoice.

->T H E  E N D.<-

<summary>This concluding page uses the writings of Augustine to argue that divine mysteries like the Trinity can only be understood through inner revelation and a preliminary "righteousness of faith." It emphasizes that one must first approach sacred text with piety and belief before full understanding and joy can be attained.</summary>

<keywords>Augustine, Trinity, Mystery of God, faith, righteousness of faith, piety, Psalm 117, spiritual enlightenment</keywords>

--- Page 248 ---
<meta>This page serves as the errata for the preceding work, which concluded with citations from Saint Augustine. It addresses typographical and linguistic errors introduced during the printing process. There is significant ghosting from the reverse side of the paper, common in books of this era where ink from one page bleeds through to the other.</meta>

## Errors, which were overlooked in haste.

Page 2, line 4, for *shows* / read *begets* <note>original: "erzeiget/ lise erzeuget." In theological texts of this period, "begetting" (generation) and "showing" (manifestation) were distinct concepts regarding the nature of Christ.</note>. line 24,
for *Enlightener-ing* / read *Enlightenment*. page 20, line 7,
for *Also* / read *Ah!* page 12, line 23, for *in the* / read *a*.
p. 25, for *be* / read *to be*. p. 30, line 3, for *flimming* /
read *Flaming*. p. 35, line 12, for *shows* / read *be-
gets*. p. 40, line 13, for *the* / read *the* <note>The original German corrects the grammatical gender from "den" to "die."</note>. p. 51, line 13,
for *shows* / read *begets*. page 59, line 1, for *the* / read *the* <note>The original corrects the case from "die" to "den."</note>.
line 31, for *ignios*, read *fiery* <note>original Latin typo: "ignios," corrected to "igneos."</note>. page 60, line 10, for
*then* / read *That*. line 17, for *escort* / read *Garment* <note>original: "Gleyt/ lise Kleid." "Gleyt" (Geleit) means protection or accompaniment, whereas "Kleid" means clothing; this likely refers to a "garment of righteousness."</note>. p. 62, line
23, for *preserve* / read *prove* <note>original: "bewahren/ lise bewåren." This changes the meaning from "to keep safe" to "to testify or confirm the truth of something."</note>. page 75, line 28, for
*saw* / read *says*. page 76, line 19, for *sees* / read *look*.
Any remaining minor printing errors, such as those concerning redundant punctuation <term>Distinction: an early modern term for the placement of punctuation marks like commas or colons</term>, or even inverted letters <note>In hand-set type, individual metal letters were often accidentally placed upside down, such as a 'u' for an 'n'.</note>,
the Christian and attentive reader will know how to correct for himself
according to his own good understanding.

<image-desc>A very faint decorative printer's ornament or watermark is visible at the bottom center of the page, appearing as a series of flourishes or a stylized floral design.</image-desc>

<summary>This page contains a list of corrections (errata) for typographical errors made during the printing process, ranging from minor grammatical slips to significant theological terms. It concludes with an appeal to the reader's intelligence to correct any remaining minor flaws in the punctuation or typesetting.</summary>

### A Secret
# TREATISE
## BY JULIUS SPERBER
### Concerning the Three
## A G E S
<note>original Latin: "SECULIS." In the 17th century, this term referred to distinct world-eras or epochs of human history.</note>

## Principal Eras / from the Beginning until the End of the World.
